L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t^{\prime})<L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)
for all
t^{\prime}>t
, i.e.,
L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)
keeps strictly decreasing in this case.

If
V_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)<0
, then
L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)=0
always holds. Assume that there exists
t_{1}<t_{2}
such that
V_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t_{1})<0
and
V_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t_{2})>0
. Due to the continuity of
V_{\lambda}
, there exists some
t\in(t_{1},t_{2})
such that
V_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)=0
. Then,
\frac{dV_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)}{dt}<-V_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)=0
, so it is strictly negative, which prevents it from becoming a positive value, so it is a contradiction. Therefore, if
L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t)=0
, then
L_{\lambda}(\pi,\bm{Q},s,t^{\prime})=0
for all
t^{\prime}>t
in this case.
∎

Therefore,
[
52
, Proposition 3.27]
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\Biggl(\max_{a_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1}}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}e_{a_{1}}^{\intercal}\widehat{Q}^{(k)}_{1,i}(s)\widehat{\pi}^{(k)}_{i}(s)\right)+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\max_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\left(e_{a_{i}}^{\intercal}\widehat{Q}^{(k)}_{i,1}(s)\widehat{\pi}^{(k)}_{1}(s)\right)-\lambda h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))\Biggr)_{+}=0

(35)

for every
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

In
8
, we will prove that an NG with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{Q}),\mathcal{A}=(\mathcal{A}_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{N}},(\widehat{Q}^{(k)}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
asymptotically becomes a zero-sum NG as
k\to\infty
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
.
Indeed, we have

\displaystyle h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))

\displaystyle=\max_{\mu\in\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left|\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{1}^{\intercal}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}_{1,i}(s)\mu_{i}+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{i}^{\intercal}\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}_{i,1}(s)\mu_{1}\right)\right|,

we can conclude that for arbitrary policy
\mu\in\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})
, the sum of payoffs in NG with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{Q}),\mathcal{A}=(\mathcal{A}_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{N}},(\widehat{Q}^{(k)}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
goes to 0 as
k\to\infty
. Therefore,
h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))\to 0
implies that the NG is zero-sum NG, where
h
is defined as
Equation

33
.

Before stating that
\widehat{Q}^{(k)}
asymptotically becomes zero-sum NGs, we state a lemma from
[
16
]
.

Lemma 1

(
[
16
]
)
.

Suppose the sequence of random variables
(y_{k})_{k\geq 0}
with
y_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies

\displaystyle y_{k+1}[n]\leq\left(1-\beta_{n,k}\right)y_{k}[n]+\beta_{n,k}\left(\gamma\left\|y_{k}\right\|_{\infty}+\bar{\epsilon}_{k}+\omega_{n,k}\right)

\displaystyle y_{k+1}[n]\geq\left(1-\beta_{n,k}\right)y_{k}[n]+\beta_{n,k}\left(-\gamma\left\|y_{k}\right\|_{\infty}+\underline{\epsilon}_{k}+\omega_{n,k}\right)

for all
k\geq 0
, where
y_{k}[n]
denotes the
n
-th element in
y_{k}
,
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\sumop\displaylimits_{k=0}^{\infty}\beta_{n,k}=\infty,\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\beta_{n,k}=0
for each
n
with probability 1, the error sequence
(\epsilon_{k})_{k\geq 0}
satisfies
\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left|\bar{\epsilon}_{k}\right|\leq c~~\text{and}~~\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left|\underline{\epsilon}_{k}\right|\leq c
for some
c\geq 0
with probability 1. Here,
\omega_{n,k}
is a stochastic approximation term that is zero-mean and has finite variance conditioned on the history. Suppose that
\left\|y_{k}\right\|_{\infty}
is bounded for all
k
. Then, we have

\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|y_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{c}{1-\gamma}

with probability 1, provided that either
\omega_{n,k}=0
for all
n,k
or
\sumop\displaylimits_{k=0}^{\infty}\beta_{n,k}^{2}<\infty
for each
n
with probability 1.

Claim 8
.

h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))
converges to 0 for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
. In other words, an NG with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{Q}),\mathcal{A}=(\mathcal{A}_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{N}},(\widehat{Q}^{(k)}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
asymptotically becomes a zero-sum NG as
k\to\infty
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

Proof.

Rewriting
Equation

35
with the belief of the value function, we have that for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)-\lambda h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))\right)_{+}=0.

(36)

By the definition of
\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)
and
Equation

36
, we have

\displaystyle-\lambda h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))\leq-h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)\leq\lambda h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s))+\bar{\epsilon}_{k}(s)

for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
k\geq 0
for some
(\bar{\epsilon}_{k}(s))_{k\geq 0}
, where
\bar{\epsilon}_{k}(s)\to 0
. Moreover, summing over all the
Q
-belief estimates over
i
, we have

\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})=(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\gamma\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in S}\mathbb{P}(s^{\prime}\mid s,\bm{a})\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s^{\prime})\right)

where
\bar{\beta}_{k}(s):=\boldsymbol{1}(s=s^{(k)})\beta^{(N(s))}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\leq(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s)})\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s)}\left(\bar{\gamma}\max_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\gamma}\bar{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\geq(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s)})\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})-\bar{\beta}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(s)}\left(\bar{\gamma}\max_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))\right)

where
\bar{\gamma}=\gamma\lambda\in(0,1)
. Since
\max_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))
is the maximal value of
\big|\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\big|
, we can apply

Lemma

1
to this situation. Let
\mathcal{Z}:={\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}
be the set of all possible state-action pairs. Then, we can view this problem as
y_{k}[z]:=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})
, for all
k\geq 0
. Note that
y_{k}
is always bounded by
2Rn/(1-\gamma)
, since the reward function is bounded by
R
, and, every timestep we update the sum of
Q_{i}
-value estimates over
i\in\mathcal{N}
with a convex combination of the previous sum of
Q_{i}
-value estimates over
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i}r_{i}(s,\bm{a})+\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}(s^{\prime}\mid s,\bm{a})V(s^{\prime})\right)\leq\left(2Rn+\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}(s^{\prime}\mid s,\bm{a})\max_{\bm{a}^{\prime}}Q(s^{\prime},\bm{a}^{\prime})\right)\leq 2Rn/(1-\gamma)
, so we can recursively show all the sum of
Q_{i}
-value estimates over
i\in\mathcal{N}
iterates are bounded. Therefore,
Lemma

1
yields that
\max_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))=\|y_{k}\|_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. As a byproduct, we also have
\lim_{k\to\infty}\big|\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\widehat{V}^{(k)}_{i}(s)\big|=0,

completing the proof.
∎

For
\bm{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}
defined in (
33
), we denote
\bm{Q}_{1}:=(Q_{1,2},\dots,Q_{1,n})
and
\bm{Q}_{-1}:=(Q_{2,1}^{\intercal},\dots,Q_{n,1}^{\intercal})^{\intercal}
. Then, we define
\text{Val}_{1}
and
\text{Val}_{-1}
, which are maxmin values with respect to
\bm{Q}_{1}
and
\bm{Q}_{-1}
, respectively, as follows:

\displaystyle\text{Val}_{1}(\bm{Q}_{1}(s))=\max_{\mu_{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{1})}\min_{\mu_{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{2}),\dots,\mu_{n}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{n})}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{1}^{\intercal}{Q}_{1,i}(s)\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\text{Val}_{-1}(\bm{Q}_{-1}(s))=\max_{\mu_{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{2}),\dots,\mu_{n}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{n})}\min_{\mu_{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{1})}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{1}^{\intercal}{Q}_{i,1}^{\intercal}(s)\mu_{i}.

Note that the
Val
operator can be viewed as the maxmin operator in the two-player zero-sum case, and it is indeed the star-shaped topology that enables us to write out a value iteration operator based on it, whose fixed point corresponds to the NE of the game. In general, it is hard to define such a value-iteration operator for other network structures. Also, note that since the maxmin formulas in
\text{Val}_{1}
and
\text{Val}_{-1}
are by definition
non-expansive
, the induced value iteration operator is
contracting
(due to the
\gamma\in(0,1)
discount factor), which is key in showing the convergence of our FP dynamics.

Claim 9
.

|\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s)-\text{Val}_{1}(\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s))|
and
|\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)-\text{Val}_{-1}(\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{-1}^{(k)}(s))|
converge to 0 for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
.

Proof.

The definition of
\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}
gives

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s)=\max_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathbb{E}_{a_{-i}\sim\widehat{\pi}_{-1}^{(k)}}\{\widehat{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\}\geq\text{Val}_{1}(\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s))\geq\min_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}\widehat{Q}_{1,i}^{(k)}(s)\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\min_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}(-(\widehat{Q}_{i,1}^{(k)}(s))^{\intercal})\mu_{i}+\min_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}(\widehat{Q}_{1,i}^{(k)}(s)+(\widehat{Q}_{i,1}^{(k)}(s))^{\intercal})\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\quad\geq-\max_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}(\widehat{Q}_{i,1}^{(k)}(s))^{\intercal}\mu_{i}+\min_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}(\widehat{Q}_{1,i}^{(k)}(s)+(\widehat{Q}_{i,1}^{(k)}(s))^{\intercal})\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\quad\geq-\max_{\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(k)})^{\intercal}(\widehat{Q}_{i,1}^{(k)}(s))^{\intercal}\mu_{i}-h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}{(s)})=-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)-h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}{(s)}),

where the third inequality is due to the summation of minimization being no greater than the minimization, and the fifth inequality is from the definition of
h
. The above inequality further implies

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s)+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s)+h(\widehat{\bm{Q}}^{(k)}{(s)})\geq\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s)-\text{Val}_{1}({\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s)})\geq 0.

The left-hand side goes to zero when
k\to\infty
, so the lemma is proved for player
1
. The other direction can be proved in the same way.
∎

Then, we define Shapley’s minimax value-iteration operator
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times(|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\})}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)}\to\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times(|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\})}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}:\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\times|\mathcal{A}_{1}|)}\to\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\times|\mathcal{A}_{1}|)}
as in a two-player zero-sum Markov game
[
13
]
that

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}\right)(s,a_{1},a_{i})=r_{1}(s,a_{1},a_{i})+\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{1}{n-1}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\text{Val}_{1}(\bm{Q}_{1}(s^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}\right)(s,a_{i},a_{1})=r_{i}(s,a_{i},a_{1})+\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{1}{n-1}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\text{Val}_{-1}(\bm{Q}_{-1}(s^{\prime})).

Also, we define several norms:

\displaystyle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\max}:\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ such that }\left\|A\right\|_{\max}=\max_{i\in[m],j\in[n]}|A_{i,j}|

\displaystyle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\max,1}:\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ such that }\left\|X_{s}\right\|_{\max,1}:=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\left\|X_{s,i}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\max,1,\max}:\mathbb{R}^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times(|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\})}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ such that }\left\|X\right\|_{\max,1}:=\left\|(\left\|X_{s}\right\|_{\max,1})_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\right\|_{\max}.

Claim 10
.

{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}
are contracting with respect to the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\max,1,\max}
.

Proof.

By definition of the Val operator, we have that for any
\bm{Q}_{1}=(Q_{1,2},\dots,Q_{1,n})\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{j}|}
and
\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}=(Q_{1,2}^{\prime},\dots,Q_{1,n}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}_{1}|\times\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{j}|}
and any
i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}
,
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1})(s,a_{1},a_{i})-({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime})(s,a_{1},a_{i})\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})(\text{Val}_{1}(\bm{Q}_{1}(s^{\prime}))-\text{Val}_{1}(\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\max_{a_{1}}\left|\max_{a_{2},\dots,a_{n}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(Q_{1,i}-Q_{1,i}^{\prime})(s^{\prime},a_{1},a_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\left\|\bm{Q}_{1}-\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max},

so for any
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
\left\|\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1})(s,a_{1},a_{i})-({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime})(s,a_{1},a_{i})\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max,1}\leq\gamma\left\|\bm{Q}_{1}-\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}
holds and therefore
\left\|\left({\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}\leq\gamma\left\|\bm{Q}_{1}-\bm{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}
.

Similarly, we have that for any
\bm{Q}_{-1}=(Q_{2,1}^{\intercal},\dots,Q_{n,1}^{\intercal})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{j}|\times|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}
and
\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}=(Q_{2,1}^{{}^{\prime}\intercal},\dots,Q_{n,1}^{{}^{\prime}\intercal})^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}|\mathcal{A}_{j}|\times|\mathcal{A}_{1}|}

\displaystyle\left\|\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1})(s,a_{i},a_{1})-({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime})(s,a_{i},a_{1})\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})(\text{Val}_{-1}(\bm{Q}_{-1}(s^{\prime}))-\text{Val}_{-1}(\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\max_{a_{2},\dots,a_{n}}\left|\max_{a_{1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}(Q_{i,1}-Q_{i,1}^{\prime})(s^{\prime},a_{i},a_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\gamma}{n-1}\left\|\bm{Q}_{-1}-\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max},

so for any
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
\left\|\left(({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1})(s,a_{i},a_{1})-({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime})(s,a_{i},a_{1})\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max,1}\leq\gamma\left\|\bm{Q}_{-1}-\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}
holds and therefore
\left\|\left({\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}-{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}\right)_{i}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}\leq\gamma\left\|\bm{Q}_{-1}-\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\prime}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}
.
∎

Since the operators
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}
are contracting, they have a unique fixed point denoted by
\bm{Q}^{\star}_{1}
and
\bm{Q}^{\star}_{-1}
. Moreover, we can guarantee that

\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q^{\star}_{1,i}(s,a_{1},a_{i})+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q^{\star}_{i,1}(s,a_{i},a_{1})=0
for every
(s,\bm{a})
and
i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}
since we can interpret it as a fixed point of zero-sum two-player Markov game and
Q^{\star}
are the
Q
value.
The update of beliefs on the
Q
-function can be written as

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})

\displaystyle=(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\widehat{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s,a_{1},a_{i})+\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\right)

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})

\displaystyle=(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{Q}_{i}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,a_{i},a_{1})+\mathcal{E}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\right).

Here,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})
and
\mathcal{E}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})=\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\left[\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s^{\prime})-\text{Val}_{1}(\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))\right]

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})=\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\widehat{V}_{i}^{(k)}(s^{\prime})-\text{Val}_{-1}(\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{-1}^{(k)}(s^{\prime}))\right]

where the two values go to 0 by
9
.

Claim 11
.

|\widehat{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q_{1}^{\star}(s,a_{1},a_{i})|
and
|\widehat{Q}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q_{-1}^{\star}(s,a_{i},a_{1})|
converge to 0 as
k\to\infty
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

Define
\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a}):=\widehat{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q_{1}^{\star}(s,a_{1},a_{i})
and
\widetilde{Q}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a}):=\widehat{Q}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q_{-1}^{\star}(s,a_{i},a_{1})
. Then, by using
Q_{1}^{\star}
and
Q_{-1}^{\star}
as the fixed point, we have that for each
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})=(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{1}^{(k)}(s,a_{1},a_{i})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}\bm{Q}_{1}^{\star}(s,a_{1}.a_{i})+\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{-1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})=(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\widetilde{Q}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\widehat{\bm{Q}}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,a_{i},a_{1})-\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}{\mathcal{T}}_{-1}\bm{Q}_{-1}^{\star}(s,a_{i},a_{1})+\mathcal{E}_{-1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})\right)

and
10
implies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\leq(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left({\gamma}\left\|\widetilde{Q}_{1}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}+\bar{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\geq(1-\bar{\beta}_{k}(s))\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k)}(s,\bm{a})+\bar{\beta}_{k}(s)\left(-{\gamma}\left\|\widetilde{Q}_{1}\right\|_{\max,1,\max}-\bar{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)

which yields
\widetilde{Q}_{1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\to 0
and also
\widetilde{Q}_{-1}^{(k+1)}(s,\bm{a})\to 0
as
k\to\infty
by
Lemma

1
.
∎

Therefore, we verified that
\widehat{V}_{1}^{(k)}(s)-\text{Val}_{1}(Q^{\star}_{1}(s))\to 0
and
\widehat{V}_{-1}^{(k)}(s)-\text{Val}_{-1}(Q^{\star}_{-1}(s))\to 0
for every
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
. Therefore, the beliefs on the opponents’ policies converge to a (perfect) Nash equilibrium of the underlying zero-sum NMG.
∎

Remark 9
.

This can be also done with the stochastic fictitious-play dynamics, in a similar way as the argument in
Section

D.1.2
.

Remark 10

(Stationary equilibrium computation via value iteration)
.

By
10
, we know that with a star-shaped topology, we can formulate a contracting value iteration operator, which plays an important role in showing the convergence of fictitious play. In fact, iterating such a contracting operator, which leads to the
value iteration
algorithm, can lead to efficient NE computation in this star-shaped case also, with a fixed constant
\gamma
. This folklore result supplements the hardness results in
Theorem

1
, where stationary equilibria computation in cases other than the star-shaped ones are computationally intractable. This completes the landscape of stationary equilibria computation in zero-sum NMGs. We provide the value-iteration process in
Algorithm

6
.
One can guarantee that
Q_{1}(s,\bm{a}):=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}Q_{1,i}(s,a_{1},a_{i})
converges to the
Q_{1}^{\star}(s,\bm{a})
, which corresponds to the Nash equilibrium values of the zero-sum NMG. Also, by solving the maxmin problem in (
37
),
Q_{1,i}(s)
provides an approximate NE policy.

Algorithm 6

Value iteration for zero-sum NMGs of a star-shape

Initialize
Q_{1,i}(s,a_{1},a_{i})=0,Q_{i,1}(s,a_{i},a_{1})=0
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}},\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A},i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}
and
V_{i}(s)=0
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
i\in\mathcal{N}

for
each iteration
t=0,1,\dots

do

Find
\mu
for each
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
such that

\displaystyle\mu(s)\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\mu_{1}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{1})}\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\mu_{2}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{2}),\dots,\mu_{n}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{n})}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{1}^{\intercal}{Q}_{1,i}(s)\mu_{i}

(37)

Update
V_{1}(s)=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}}\mu_{1}^{\intercal}(s){Q}_{1,i}(s)\mu_{i}(s)
for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}}

Update
Q_{1,i}(s,a_{1},a_{i})=r_{1,i}(s,a_{1},a_{i})+\gamma\sumop\displaylimits_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{1}{n-1}\mathbb{P}_{1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a_{1})V_{1}(s^{\prime})
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}/\{1\}
,
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
\bm{a}\in\mathcal{A}

end

for

Appendix E
Background on Stochastic Approximation and Differential Inclusions

Appendix

E
introduces the theorem statement of
[
52
]
.
Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightrightarrows\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a set-valued function. Assume that
F
satisfies the following properties:

1.

F
is a closed set-valued map, meaning that its graph
\operatorname{Graph}(F)=\{(x,y):y\in F(x)\}
is a closed subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

2.

F(x)
is a non-empty, compact, and convex subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

3.

There exists a constant
c>0
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we have
\sup_{z\in F(x)}|z|\leq c(1+|x|)
.

The differential inclusion problem involves finding a solution vector function
\bm{x}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
that satisfies the initial condition
\bm{x}(0)=x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and the following relationship for almost all
t\in\mathbb{R}
:

\frac{d\bm{x}(t)}{dt}\in F(\bm{x}(t)).

Definition 7

(Perturbed solutions)
.

A perturbed solution to
F
refers to a continuous function
\bm{y}:\mathbb{R}_{+}=[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{m}
that meets the following requirements:

•

\bm{y}
is absolutely continuous.

•

There is a locally integrable function
t\mapsto U(t)
that satisfies:

–

For all
T>0
, the supremum of
\left|\intop\nolimits_{t}^{t+v}U(s)ds\right|
over the interval
0\leq v\leq T
converges to zero as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

–

For almost every
t>0
, the expression
\frac{d\bm{y}(t)}{dt}-U(t)
belongs to
F^{\delta(t)}(\bm{y}(t))
, where
\delta:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a function such that
\delta(t)\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

Definition 8

(Stochastic approximations)
.

A discrete-time process
\left\{x_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a stochastic approximation if it satisfies the following relationship:

x_{n+1}-x_{n}-\gamma_{n+1}U_{n+1}\in\gamma_{n+1}F\left(x_{n}\right),

where the characteristics
\gamma
and
U
meet the following conditions:

•

The sequence
(\gamma_{n})_{n\geq 1}
consists of non-negative numbers such that
\sumop\displaylimits_{n=1}^{\infty}\gamma_{n}=\infty
and
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\gamma_{n}=0
.

•

The elements
U_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
can be either deterministic or random perturbations.

A continuous-time process can be associated with such a process as follows:

Definition 9

(Affine interpolated process)
.

Define the following:

\tau_{0}=0\text{ and }\tau_{n}=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\text{ for }n\geq 1.

The continuous-time
affine interpolated process

\bm{w}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is defined as:

\bm{w}\left(\tau_{n}+s\right):=x_{n}+s\frac{x_{n+1}-x_{n}}{\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n}},\quad s\in\left[0,\gamma_{n+1}\right).

We define
\Phi_{t}(x)=\{\bm{x}(t):\bm{x}
is a solution to
\frac{d\bm{x}(t)}{dt}\in F(\bm{x}(t))
with
\bm{x}(0)=x\}
.

Definition 10

(Lyapunov function)
.

Lyapunov function for a set
{\mathcal{S}}
is a continuous function
V:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}
if
V(y)<V(x)
for all
x\in{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
y\in\Phi_{t}(x),t>0
, and
V(y)\leq V(x)
for all
x\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
y\in\Phi_{t}(x),t>0
.

Theorem 6
.

Assume that the following hold:

•

For all
T>0
, the supremum of
\left\|\sumop\displaylimits_{i=n}^{k-1}\gamma_{i+1}U_{i+1}\right\|
for
k=n+1,\ldots,m\left(\tau_{n}+T\right)
converges to zero as
n\to\infty
, where

m(t)=\sup\left\{k\geq 0:t\geq\tau_{k}\right\}.

•

\sup_{n}\left\|x_{n}\right\|=M<\infty
.

Then the affine interpolated process (c.f.
Definition

9
) is a perturbed solution.

Theorem 7
.

Suppose that
V
is a Lyapunov function for a set
\Lambda
. Assume that
V(\Lambda)
has an empty interior. For every bounded perturbed solution
\bm{y}
, define
L(\bm{y})=\bigcapop\displaylimits_{t\geq 0}\overline{\{\bm{y}(s):s\geq t\}}
, then
L(\mathbf{y})
is contained in
\Lambda
and
V(L(\mathbf{y}))
is constant.

Appendix F
Omitted Details in
Section

6

We first recall the folklore result that approximating Markov non-stationary NE in
infinite-horizon
discounted settings can be achieved by finding approximate Markov NE in
finite-horizon
settings, with a large enough horizon length.

Proposition 10
.

A
2\epsilon
-approximate Markov non-stationary NE in an infinite-horizon
\gamma
-discounted MG can be generated by (1) truncating the trajectory at time step
H\geq\frac{\log(R/\epsilon)}{1-\gamma}
and (2) finding an
\epsilon
-approximate Markov NE in the
H
-horizon MG.

Proof.

We will execute a policy
\pi
such that (1) for the first
H
steps, we follow
\epsilon
-approximate NE in the
H
-truncated MG, and (2) after the
H
steps, we follow an arbitrary policy.
Then, we have

\displaystyle V^{\pi}_{i}(s)=\sumop\displaylimits_{h=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{i},\pi_{-i}}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]-\epsilon+\sumop\displaylimits_{h=H+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\pi}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{i},\pi_{-i}}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]-\epsilon+\sumop\displaylimits_{h=H+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{i},\pi_{-i}}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]-\frac{R\gamma^{h-1}}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\geq\sumop\displaylimits_{h=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{i},\pi_{-i}}[\gamma^{h-1}r_{i}(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}=s]-2\epsilon

for an arbitrary policy
\mu_{i}
. Here, the first inequality comes from the definition of NE in the
H
-truncated MG, the second inequality comes from
0\leq r_{i}\leq R
and the last inequality comes from the definition of
H
. Therefore, the executed policy is a 2
\epsilon
-approximate NE.
∎

In this section, we will utilize two performance metrics: Matrix-NE-Gap and Matrix-QRE-Gap. For the definition of QRE, we refer to
Definition

6
and (
30
).

Definition 11
.

For an NG
M
with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{r}),\mathcal{A}=\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{A}_{i},(r_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{r}})
, we define Matrix-NE-Gap and
Matrix-QRE-Gap
of
M
for some product policy
\pi
as follows:

\displaystyle\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(M,\pi)=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(r_{i}(\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i})-r_{i}(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}\right)\right].

\displaystyle\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(M,\pi)=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(r_{\tau,i}(\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i})-r_{\tau,i}(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)\right].

When the underlying graph and the action space for the NG are clear, we also write
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(M,\pi)
or
\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(M,\pi)
as
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{r},\pi)
or
\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(\bm{r},\pi)
, respectively.

We now provide the relationship between
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{r},\pi)
and
\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(\bm{r},\pi)
.

Lemma 2

(
[
53
]
, Page 6, Equation (8))
.

For an NG
M
with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{r}),\mathcal{A}=\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{A}_{i},(r_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{r}})
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{r},\pi)\leq\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(\bm{r},\pi)+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|.

Thus, setting
\tau=\frac{\epsilon}{2\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}
, then an
\epsilon/2
-approximate QRE is also an
\epsilon
-approximate NE. Hence, finding an approximate-QRE for zero-sum NGs is sufficient for finding an approximate-NE, with a small enough
\tau
. Now, we define the NE-Gap for an MNMG.

Definition 12
.

For an MNMG
M
with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{Q}),{\mathcal{S}},\mathcal{A},H,(\mathbb{P}_{h})_{h\in[H]},(r_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q},s\in{\mathcal{S}}})
, NE-Gap for some product policy
\pi
at timestep
h\in[H]
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h}(M,\pi)

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times H}}\left(V_{h,i}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{\pi}(s)\right).

Now we summarize the algorithm in
Algorithm

2
. By
Algorithm

2
,
Q_{h,i,j}(s,a_{i},a_{j})=Q_{\tau,h,i,j}^{{\pi}}(s,a_{i},a_{j})
and
V_{h,i}(s)=V_{\tau,h,i}^{{\pi}}(s)
holds, so that an NG characterized by
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(Q_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
is always a zero-sum NG for all
s\in{\mathcal{S}},h\in[H]
. And by induction, we can show that

|Q_{h,i,j}^{\pi}(s,a_{i},a_{j})|\leq(H+1-h)R
for all
\pi
,
h\in[H]
,
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}
,
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
,
a_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
(i.e.,
\left\|\bm{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(s)\right\|_{\max}\leq HR
for all
\pi
,
h\in[H]
,
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
).
See
4

Proof.

Let
M=(\mathcal{G},{\mathcal{S}},\mathcal{A},H,(\mathbb{P}_{h})_{h\in[H]},(r_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q},s\in{\mathcal{S}}})
. For any
\pi
and
h\in[H]
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h}(M,\pi)=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times H}}\left(V_{h,i}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{\pi_{i}}(s)\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times H}}\left[V_{h,i}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{(\pi_{h,i}^{\prime},\pi_{h,-i}),\pi_{h+1:H}}(s)+V_{h,i}^{(\pi_{h,i}^{\prime},\pi_{h,-i}),\pi_{h+1:H}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{\pi}(s)\right]

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})^{|{\mathcal{S}}|\times H}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{h,(\pi^{\prime}_{h,i},\pi_{h,-i})}(V_{h+1,i}^{\pi_{i}^{\prime},\pi_{-i}}-V_{h+1,i}^{\pi_{h+1:H}})(s)+V_{h,i}^{(\pi_{h,i}^{\prime},\pi_{h,-i}),\pi_{h+1:H}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{\pi}(s)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h+1}(M,\pi)+\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\max_{\pi_{h,i}^{\prime}(s)\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[V_{h,i}^{(\pi_{h,i}^{\prime},\pi_{h,-i}),\pi_{h+1:H}}(s)-V_{h,i}^{\pi}(s)\right]

\displaystyle=\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h+1}(M,\pi)+\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{Q}_{h}(s),\pi_{h})\leq\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h+1}(M,\pi)+\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\epsilon_{h,s}.

Therefore, for any
\pi
and
h\in[H]
, we have
\textsf{NE-Gap}_{h}(M,\pi)\leq\sumop\displaylimits_{h\in[H]}\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\epsilon_{h,s}
.
∎

F.1
Several examples of NE-ORACLE

Example 1. Optimism & Regularization: OMWU algorithm
[
53
]
.

According to
[
53
, Theorem 1]
, if we apply
Algorithm

7
to
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(Q_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
for each
h\in[H],s\in{\mathcal{S}}
,
the required number of iterations for
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(s),\pi_{h})\leq\epsilon/H
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{2}d_{\max}}/{\epsilon}\right)
where
d_{\max}
is the maximum degree of underlying graph
\mathcal{G}
. Consequently, the overall iteration complexity is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{3}d_{\max}|{\mathcal{S}}|}/{\epsilon}\right)
. Note that these results are in terms of last-iterate convergence.

Algorithm 7

OMWU for zero-sum NGs with
\tau
-entropy regularization
[
53
]

Choose
\pi_{i}^{(0)},\bar{\pi}_{i}^{(0)}
as uniform distributions for all
i\in\mathcal{N}

Define
\tau=1/(n\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)
and
\eta={1}/({8n\left\|\bm{r}\right\|_{\infty}})

for
timestep
t=0,1,\dots,

do

Update the policy
\bar{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}
as
\bar{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}(a_{i})\propto\bar{\pi}^{(t)}_{i}(a_{i})^{1-\eta\tau}\exp\left(\eta[\bm{r}_{i}{\pi}^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}

Update the policy
{\pi}^{(t+1)}_{i}
as
{\pi}^{(t+1)}_{i}(a_{i})\propto\bar{\pi}^{(t+1)}_{i}(a_{i})^{1-\eta\tau}\exp\left(\eta[\bm{r}_{i}{\pi}^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}

end

for

[
54
]
.

According to
[
54
, Theorem 3.4]
, if we apply

8
to
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(Q_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
for each
h\in[H],s\in{\mathcal{S}}
, the required number of iterations for
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(s),\pi_{h})\leq\epsilon/H
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{3}n}/{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
. Consequently, the overall iteration complexity is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{4}n|{\mathcal{S}}|}/{\epsilon^{2}}\right)
. Note that these results are in terms of best-iterate convergence.

OMD with KL-distance generating function for zero-sum NGs

\pi_{i}^{(0)},\bar{\pi}_{i}^{(0)}

for

and

Example 3. Optimism-free & Regularization: MWU algorithm.

We provide MWU for zero-sum NGs with regularization in
Section

F.2
. According to
Theorem

8
and
Theorem

9
, if we apply
Algorithm

9
or
Algorithm

10
to
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(Q_{h,i,j}(s))_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{Q}})
for each
h\in[H],s\in{\mathcal{S}}
, the required number of iterations for
\textsf{Matrix-NE-Gap}(\bm{Q}_{h}^{\pi}(s),\pi_{h})\leq\epsilon/H
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{8}n}/{\epsilon^{4}}\right)
or
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{18}n^{3}}/{\epsilon^{6}}\right)
, respectively. Consequently, the overall iteration complexity is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{9}n|{\mathcal{S}}|}/{\epsilon^{4}}\right)
or
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({H^{19}n^{3}|{\mathcal{S}}|}/{\epsilon^{6}}\right)
, respectively. Note that these results are in terms of last-iterate convergence.

F.2
Analysis of MWU for zero-sum NGs with regularization

F.2.1
Fixed regularization

First, we provide an algorithm that has a
fixed
coefficient for entropy-regularization (
Algorithm

9
). Recall that
0\leq r_{i}\leq R
for some
R>0
, for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
.

Algorithm 9

MWU for zero-sum NGs with
\tau
-entropy regularization

Choose
K=\left\lceil 2R/\tau+\log(\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|))\right\rceil

Choose
{\pi}_{i}^{(0)}
as a uniform distribution for all
i\in\mathcal{N}

for
timestep
t=0,1,\dots

do

Define
\eta^{(t)}=1/(\tau(t+K))

Update the policy as
{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}(a_{i})\propto(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\exp\left(\eta^{(t)}[\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}

end

for

Claim 12
.

Define
{}_{i}=\left\{\pi_{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})\mid\pi_{i}(a_{i})\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)\text{ for all }a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}
and
g_{i}^{(t)}=\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}-\tau\log\pi_{i}^{(t)}
in
Algorithm

9
. Then,
{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\pi_{i}\in{}_{i}}\left(\pi_{i}^{\intercal}g_{i}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{\eta^{(t)}}\text{KL}(\pi_{i},{\pi}_{i}^{(t)})\right)
holds for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

The equation
{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}(a_{i})\propto(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\exp\left(\eta^{(t)}[\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)
implies that

{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\pi_{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\pi_{i}^{\intercal}g_{i}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{\eta^{(t)}}\text{KL}(\pi_{i},{\pi}_{i}^{(t)})\right)

by a simple algebra. The remaining part to establish is that
\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\in{}_{i}
holds true for all
t\geq 0
and for every
i\in\mathcal{N}
. To prove the remaining part, we use induction. As
\pi_{i}^{(0)}
is chosen to be a uniform distribution, it is clear that
\pi_{i}^{(0)}\in{}_{i}
. Under the assumption that
\pi_{i}^{(t)}\in{}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}(a_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\exp\left([\eta^{(t)}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)}{\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}^{\prime}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\exp\left([\eta^{(t)}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\geq}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)\right)^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}}{\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}^{\prime}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\exp\left([\eta^{(t)}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\geq}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)\right)^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}}{\exp(\eta^{(t)}R)\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}^{\prime}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}}\underset{(iii)}{\geq}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)\right)^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}}{\exp(\eta^{(t)}R)|\mathcal{A}_{i}|^{\eta^{(t)}\tau}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)

thereby concluding the proof of our claim. In the above,
(i)
is derived from
\exp\left([\eta^{(t)}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)\geq 1
and the induction hypothesis;
(ii)
is the result of
\exp\left([\eta^{(t)}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}]_{a_{i}}\right)\leq\exp(\eta^{(t)}R)
. Finally,
(iii)
comes from
\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}(\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i}))^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}\leq|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\right)^{1-\eta^{(t)}\tau}
, by Jensen’s inequality.
∎

In the forthcoming analysis of
Algorithm

9
and
Algorithm

10
, our first step bounds
\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi)
. Following this, we employ
Proposition

11
below to bound the term
\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}
. Finally, we leverage
Lemma

2
to bound the
Matrix-NE-Gap
.

Proposition 11
.

For any
\tau>0
,
\pi
, and
\bm{r}
, the following holds:

\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(\bm{r},\pi)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+R\right)\sqrt{\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi)}\right).

Proof.

For any
\pi
,
\tau
, and
\bm{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{Matrix-QRE-Gap}_{\tau}(\bm{r},\pi)

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\Bigg[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})+\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}-\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)+\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}R\left\|\pi_{j}-\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}\right\|_{1}-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}r_{i,j}\pi_{j}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\Bigg[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}R\left\|\pi_{j}-\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}\right\|_{1}+R\left\|\pi_{i}-\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star}\right\|_{1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\underset{(iii)}{\leq}\mathcal{O}\left(R\sqrt{\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi)}\right)+\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\underset{(iv)}{\leq}\mathcal{O}\left(R\sqrt{\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\Biggl[\max_{\pi_{i}^{\prime}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{i}^{\prime}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star}r_{i,j}\pi_{\tau,j}^{\star}+\tau\mathcal{H}(\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star})\right)+\sqrt{3}\tau\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|\sqrt{\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau,i}^{\star},\pi_{i})}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+R\right)\sqrt{\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi)}\right),

where
(i)
and
(ii)
are due to the definition of
R
. Meanwhile,
(iii)
holds by the Pinsker inequality, and
(iv)
holds by bounding the difference in Shannon entropy via KL divergence,
[
117
, Theorem 2]
.
∎

Lemma 3

(
[
94
,
53
]
)
.

For any zero-sum NG with
(\mathcal{G}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{r}),\mathcal{A},(r_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{r}})
, for any joint product policies
\mu,\nu
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left(r_{i}(\mu_{i},\nu_{-i})+r_{i}(\nu_{i},\mu_{-i})\right)=0.

By
Lemma

3
and the definition of
r_{\tau,i}
(
Equation

30
), we can derive

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left(r_{\tau,i}(\pi_{i}^{(t)},\pi^{\star}_{\tau,-i})+r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)})\right)=0.

(38)

Analogous to the method used in
12
, we can show that the QRE,
\pi^{\star}_{\tau}
, belongs to
\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}{}_{i}
. Furthermore, we can bound
g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})

\displaystyle\leq R-\tau\log{\pi}_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq R-\tau\log\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|}\exp\left(-\frac{R}{\tau}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=2R+\tau\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)

so that
\eta^{(t)}g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq 1
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
,
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, and
t\geq 0
. Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

4
below.

Lemma 4

(
[
118
]
, Theorem 2)
.

For a convex set
\Omega\subseteq\Delta(\mathcal{A})
,
\eta\mu\preceq\boldsymbol{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
, and any
\pi\in\Omega
, define

\pi^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\widetilde{\pi}\in\Omega}\left({\widetilde{\pi}}^{\intercal}\mu-\frac{1}{\eta}\text{KL}(\widetilde{\pi},\pi)\right).

Then, for any
\nu\in\Omega
, the following holds:

\displaystyle(\nu-\pi)^{\intercal}g\leq\frac{\text{KL}(\nu,\pi)-\text{KL}(\nu,\pi^{\prime})}{\eta}+\eta\sumop\displaylimits_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\pi(a)g(a)^{2}.

Now, we state the theorem on the iteration complexity of
Algorithm

9
to obtain an
\epsilon
-NE.

Theorem 8
.

The last iterate of
Algorithm

9
requires no more than
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(nR^{4}/\epsilon^{4}\right)
iterations to achieve an
\epsilon
-NE of
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(r_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{r}})
.

Proof.

First, we bound the difference between
r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)})
and
r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{(t)})
as follows:

\displaystyle r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)})-r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i}-\pi^{(t)}_{i}\right)^{\intercal}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}+\tau\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\log\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})-\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i}(a_{i})\log\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i}(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i}-\pi^{(t)}_{i}\right)^{\intercal}g_{i}^{(t)}-\tau\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)}{\eta^{(t)}}+\eta^{(t)}\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\left(g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\right)^{2}-\tau\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)}{\eta^{(t)}}+\eta^{(t)}\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}-\tau\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right).

Here, the penultimate inequality holds by
Lemma

4
since
\pi_{\tau,i}\in{}_{i}
and
\eta^{(t)}g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq 1
holds for all
t>0
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)+\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{(t)})-r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)}))+(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}

(39)

for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
t\geq 0
. If we sum (
39
) over
i\in\mathcal{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{(t)})-r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau,i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)}))

\displaystyle=\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau,i}(\pi_{i}^{(t)},\pi^{\star}_{\tau,-i})-r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau}))

(40)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}

(41)

for all
t\geq 0
where (
40
) holds due to (
38
) and
\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{(t)})=\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}r_{\tau,i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau})=0
, and (
41
) holds due to that
\pi^{\star}_{\tau}
is the NE for the game that having the payoff
r_{\tau,i}
.
If we recursively apply (
41
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prodop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta^{(l)}\tau)\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})+n\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(\eta^{(l)})^{2}\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-\eta^{(s)}\tau)

\displaystyle=\prodop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1-1/(l+K))\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})

\displaystyle\qquad+n\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}(1/(l+K)^{2})\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-1/(s+K))

\displaystyle\leq K/(K+t)\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})

\displaystyle\qquad+n\left(2R+\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\log\left((t+K+1)/(K)\right)(1/(t+K))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(K\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)+n\max\left(R^{2}/\tau^{2},\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log^{2}(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)\right)/t\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(n\max\left(R^{2}/\tau^{2},\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log^{2}(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)/t\right).

Therefore, if we iterate
Algorithm

9
for
T
times, by
Proposition

11
and
Lemma

2
, we obtain an

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+\left(\tau\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+R\right)\sqrt{n\max\left(R^{2}/\tau^{2},\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log^{2}(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)\right)/T}\right)

approximate NE. Therefore, if we want to obtain an
\epsilon
-NE (
\epsilon>0
) for the matrix game in the last iterate, we need to have
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(nR^{4}/\epsilon^{4}\right)
iteration.
∎

F.2.2
Diminishing regularization

One might also consider the algorithm with a diminishing choice of
\tau
. We provide
Algorithm

10
for this case.

Algorithm 10

MWU for zero-sum NGs with diminishing entropy regularization

Choose
K=(R+2\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|)^{2}

Choose
{\pi}_{i}^{(0)}
as a uniform distribution for all
i\in\mathcal{N}

for
timestep
t=0,1,\dots

do

Define
\tau^{(t)}=(t+K)^{-1/6}
and
\eta^{(t)}=(t+K)^{-1/2}

Update
g_{i}^{(t)}=\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}-\tau^{(t)}\log{\pi}_{i}^{(t)}
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}

Define
{}_{i}^{(t)}=\left\{\pi_{i}\in\Delta(\mathcal{A}_{i})\mid\pi_{i}(a_{i})\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K)^{2}}\text{ for all }a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\}

Update the policy as
{\pi}_{i}^{(t+1)}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\pi_{i}\in{}_{i}^{(t)}}\left(\pi_{i}^{\intercal}g_{i}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{\eta^{(t)}}\text{KL}(\pi_{i},{\pi}_{i}^{(t)})\right)
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}

end

for

Let
\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}}
be the unique NE in the policy space
\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}{}_{i}^{(t)}
for the game that has the payoff
r_{\tau^{(t)},i}
as

\displaystyle r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi)

\displaystyle=r_{i}(\pi)+\tau^{(t)}\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})-\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\frac{\tau^{(t)}}{|\mathcal{E}_{r,j}|}\mathcal{H}(\pi_{j})=\pi_{i}^{\intercal}\bm{r}_{i}\pi+\tau^{(t)}\mathcal{H}(\pi_{i})-\sumop\displaylimits_{j\in\mathcal{E}_{r,i}}\frac{\tau^{(t)}}{|\mathcal{E}_{r,j}|}\mathcal{H}(\pi_{j}).

Moreover, we can bound
g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
:

\displaystyle g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})

\displaystyle\leq R-(t+K)^{-1/6}\log{\pi}_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))

\displaystyle\leq R+2\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|),

so that
\eta^{(t)}g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq 1
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
,
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, and
t\geq 0
. Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

4
.

Theorem 9
.

The last iterate of
Algorithm

10
requires no more than
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(n^{3}R^{12}/\epsilon^{6}\right)
iterations to achieve an
\epsilon
-NE of
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A},(r_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}_{r}})
.

Proof.

First, we bound the difference between
r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)})
and
r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{(t)})
as follows:

\displaystyle r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)})-r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i}-\pi^{(t)}_{i}\right)^{\intercal}\bm{r}_{i}\pi^{(t)}+\tau^{(t)}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\log\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})-\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i}(a_{i})\log\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i}(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i}-\pi^{(t)}_{i}\right)^{\intercal}g_{i}^{(t)}-\tau^{(t)}\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)}{\eta^{(t)}}

\displaystyle+\eta^{(t)}\sumop\displaylimits_{a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\pi_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\left(g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\right)^{2}-\tau^{(t)}\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)}{\eta^{(t)}}

\displaystyle+\eta^{(t)}\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}-\tau^{(t)}\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right).

Here, the penultimate inequality holds by
Lemma

4
since
\pi_{\tau^{(t)},i}\in{}_{i}^{(t)}
and
\eta^{(t)}g_{i}^{(t)}(a_{i})\leq 1
holds for every
t>0
and
a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau^{(t)}\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t)}\right)+\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{(t)})-r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)}))

(42)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{i}^{(t+1)}\right)

for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
and
t\geq 0
. If we sum (
42
) over
i\in\mathcal{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau^{(t)}\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{(t)})-r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},i},\pi_{-i}^{(t)}))+\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau^{(t)}\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}

(43)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sumop\displaylimits_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\eta^{(t)}(r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi_{i}^{(t)},\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)},-i})-r_{\tau^{(t)},i}(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}}))+\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta^{(t)}\tau^{(t)}\right)\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t)}\right)+n(\eta^{(t)})^{2}\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right),

(44)

for all
t\geq 0
where (
43
) holds due to (
38
), and (
44
) holds due to that
\pi^{\star}_{\tau}
is the NE for the game with the payoff
r_{\tau^{(t)},i}
. If we recursively apply (
44
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prodop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta^{(l)}\tau^{(l)})\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})+n\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(\eta^{(l)})^{2}\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-\eta^{(s)}\tau^{(s)})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-\eta^{(s)}\tau^{(s)})\left(\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prodop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1-(t+K)^{-2/3})\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})+n\left(R+2(t+K)^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))\right)^{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1/(l+K))\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-(s+K)^{-2/3})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-(s+K)^{-2/3})\left(\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(n\max(R^{2},\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|t)t^{-1/3})(t+K)^{-1/3}+\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log^{3}(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|t)(t+K)^{-1/3}\right)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(nR^{2}t^{-1/3}\right).

Here, (i) holds because

\displaystyle\prodop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1-(t+K)^{-2/3})\text{KL}(\pi_{\tau}^{\star},\pi^{(0)})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\exp\left(-t^{1/3}\right)\right)

holds,

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}(1/(l+K))\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-(s+K)^{-2/3})=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}((t+K)^{-1/3})

holds by
[
119
, Lemma 4]
, and

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{l=0}^{t}\prodop\displaylimits_{s=l+1}^{t}(1-(s+K)^{-2/3})\left(\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t+1)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)-\mathrm{KL}\left(\pi^{\star}_{\tau^{(t)}},\pi^{(t+1)}\right)\right)\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log^{3}(|\mathcal{A}_{i}|(t+K))(t+K)^{-1/3}

holds by
[
119
, Lemma 4, Lemma 15]
. Therefore, if we iterate
Algorithm

10
for
T
times, by
Proposition

11
and
Lemma

2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(T^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+\left(T^{-1/6}\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+R\right)\sqrt{nR^{2}T^{-1/3}}\right)

approximate NE. Therefore, if we want to obtain
\epsilon
-NE (
\epsilon>0
) for the matrix game, we need to have
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(n^{3}R^{12}/\epsilon^{6}\right)
iterations.
∎
